```falcon
local cities = [{"city":"Delhi","temp":42},{"city":"Mumbai","temp":35},{"city":"Bangalore","temp":28},{"city":"Chennai","temp":38},{"city":"Kolkata","temp":40}]
local top3 = []

local best = 0
 
local bestIdx = 1
  for (j: 1 .. cities.listLen()) {
 
local inTop = false
    for (t in top3) {
      if (t === cities[j].get("city","")) {
        inTop = true
```

Write `resolveCollisions(entities)` — detect and respond to pairwise collisions (circle vs circle). Return updated list.
```falcon
func resolveCollisions(entities) = {
 
local updated = copyList(entities)
  for (i: 1 .. updated.listLen()) {
    for (j: i+1 .. updated.listLen()) {
 
local a = updated[i]
 
local b = updated[j]
 
local dx = b.get("x",0) - a.get("x",0)
 
local dy = b.get("y",0) - a.get("y",0)
 
local dist = sqrt(dx*dx+dy*dy)
 
local minDist = a.get("r",0) + b.get("r",0)
      if (dist < minDist && dist > 0) {
 
local overlap = (minDist - dist) / 2
 
local nx = dx / dist
 
local ny = dy / dist
 
local a2 = copyDict(a)
 
local b2 = copyDict(b)
        a2.set("x", a.get("x",0) - nx * overlap)
        a2.set("y", a.get("y",0) - ny * overlap)
        b2.set("x", b.get("x",0) + nx * overlap)
        b2.set("y", b.get("y",0) + ny * overlap)
        updated[i] = a2
        updated[j] = b2
      }
    }
  }
  updated
}
local ents = [{"id":1,"x":0,"y":0,"r":5},{"id":2,"x":4,"y":0,"r":5}]
println(resolveCollisions(ents))
```

Write `computeScore(kills, time, deaths, combo)` — formula combining all factors. Return final score and letter grade.
```falcon
func computeScore(kills, time, deaths, combo) = {
 
local base = kills * 100
 
local timebonus = if (time < 60) 500 else if (time < 120) 200 else 0
 
local deathpenalty = deaths * 50
 
local combobonus = combo * combo * 10
 
local score = base + timebonus - deathpenalty + combobonus
 
local grade = if (score >= 1500) "S" else if (score >= 1000) "A" else if (score >= 700) "B" else if (score >= 400) "C" else "D"
  {"score": score, "grade": grade}
}
println(computeScore(10, 45, 1, 5))
```

Write `applyStatusEffects(entity, effects)` — apply poison, burn, freeze effects from a list of `{type, magnitude, duration}` dicts. Return modified entity.
```falcon
func applyStatusEffects(entity, effects) = {
 
local e = copyDict(entity)
  for (effect in effects) {
 
local type = effect.get("type","")
 
local mag = effect.get("magnitude",0)
    if (type === "poison") {
      e.set("hp", e.get("hp",0) - mag)
      e.set("poisoned", true)
    } else if (type === "burn") {
      e.set("hp", e.get("hp",0) - mag * 1.5)
      e.set("burning", true)
    } else if (type === "freeze") {
      e.set("speed", e.get("speed",0) - mag)
      e.set("frozen", true)
    }
  }
  e
}
local entity = {"hp": 100, "speed": 10}
local effects = [{"type":"poison","magnitude":5,"duration":3},{"type":"freeze","magnitude":3,"duration":2}]
println(applyStatusEffects(entity, effects))
```

Write `objectiveTracker(objectives)` — list of `{name, complete, required}`. Write `completeObj(name)`, `allDone()`, `nextObjective()`. Test.
```falcon
global objectives = []

func objectiveTracker(objs) {
  this.objectives = objs
}

func completeObj(name) {
  for (i: 1 .. this.objectives.listLen()) {
    if (this.objectives[i].get("name","") === name) {
      this.objectives[i].set("complete", true)
    }
  }
}

func allDone() = {
 
local done = true
  for (obj in this.objectives) {
    if (obj.get("required",false) && !obj.get("complete",false)) {
      done = false
    }
  }
  done
}

func nextObjective() = {
 
local result = "None"
  for (obj in this.objectives) {
    if (!obj.get("complete",false) && result === "None") {
      result = obj.get("name","")
    }
  }
  result
}

objectiveTracker([{"name":"kill_boss","complete":false,"required":true},{"name":"find_key","complete":false,"required":true},{"name":"bonus_chest","complete":false,"required":false}])
completeObj("kill_boss")
println(allDone())
println(nextObjective())
```

Write `buildTileset(rules)` — given a list of tile placement rules, generate a valid 5×5 tilemap. Return the grid.
```falcon
func buildTileset(rules) = {
 
local grid = []
  for (r: 1 .. 5) {
 
local row = []
    for (c: 1 .. 5) {
 
local tile = "grass"
      for (rule in rules) {
 
local rr = rule.get("row", -1)
 
local rc = rule.get("col", -1)
        if ((rr == -1 || rr == r) && (rc == -1 || rc == c)) {
          tile = rule.get("tile", "grass")
        }
      }
      row.add(tile)
    }
    grid.add(row)
  }
  grid
}
local rules = [{"row": 1, "tile": "water"}, {"col": 5, "tile": "wall"}, {"row": 3, "col": 3, "tile": "chest"}]
println(buildTileset(rules))
```

```falcon
global gameState = {}

func buildEventChain(events) = {
 
local chain = {}
  for (ev in events) {
    chain.set(ev.get("trigger",""), ev)
  }
  chain
}

func runChain(start, gs) = {
 
local chain = buildEventChain([{"trigger":"start","action":"openDoor","next":"doorOpen"},{"trigger":"doorOpen","action":"spawnEnemy","next":"fight"},{"trigger":"fight","action":"endGame","next":""}])
 
local cur = start
 
local state = copyDict(gs)
  while (cur !== "") {
 
local ev = chain.get(cur, {})
 
local action = ev.get("action","")
    state.set("lastAction", action)
    println("Running: " _ action)
    cur = ev.get("next","")
  }
  state
}
```

Write `simulateGrowth(ecosystem, ticks)` — predator-prey Lotka-Volterra step. Print population each tick.
```falcon
func simulateGrowth(ecosystem, ticks) {
 
local prey = ecosystem.get("prey", 100)
 
local predator = ecosystem.get("predator", 10)
 
local alpha = 0.1
 
local beta = 0.02
 
local gamma = 0.3
 
local delta = 0.01
  for (t: 1 .. ticks) {
 
local newPrey = prey + (alpha * prey - beta * prey * predator)
 
local newPredator = predator + (delta * prey * predator - gamma * predator)
    prey = newPrey
    predator = newPredator
    println("Tick " _ t _ ": prey=" _ round(prey) _ " predator=" _ round(predator))
  }
}
simulateGrowth({"prey": 100, "predator": 10}, 10)
```

Write `circleContains(cx, cy, r, px, py)` — true if point is inside circle. Test 3 cases.
```falcon
func circleContains(cx, cy, r, px, py) = {
 
local dx = px - cx
 
local dy = py - cy
  dx * dx + dy * dy < r * r
}
```

Write `closestPointOnSegment(px, py, ax, ay, bx, by)` — project point onto segment, clamp to [0,1]. Return `[x, y]`.
```falcon
func closestPointOnSegment(px, py, ax, ay, bx, by) = {
 
local dx = bx - ax
 
local dy = by - ay
 
local lenSq = dx * dx + dy * dy
 
local t = 0
  if (lenSq != 0) {
    t = ((px - ax) * dx + (py - ay) * dy) / lenSq
    t = if (t < 0) 0 else if (t > 1) 1 else t
  }
 
local listResult = [ax + t * dx, ay + t * dy]
  listResult
}
println(closestPointOnSegment(3, 0, 0, 0, 5, 0))
println(closestPointOnSegment(6, 0, 0, 0, 5, 0))
println(closestPointOnSegment(2, 3, 0, 0, 4, 0))
```

Write `tspGreedy(dist_matrix, n)` — greedy nearest-neighbor TSP. Test 4 cities.
```falcon
func tspGreedy(dist_matrix, n) = {
 
local visited = [1]
 
local tour = [1]
  while (visited.listLen() < n) {
 
local last = tour[tour.listLen()]
 
local best = 999999
 
local bestNode = -1
    for (j: 1 .. n) {
      if (!visited.containsItem(j) && dist_matrix[last][j] < best) {
        best = dist_matrix[last][j]
        bestNode = j
      }
    }
    visited.add(bestNode)
    tour.add(bestNode)
  }
  tour.add(1)
  tour
}
local dm = [[0,10,15,20],[10,0,35,25],[15,35,0,30],[20,25,30,0]]
println(tspGreedy(dm, 4))
```

Write `graphSignature(graph)` — sorted list of (degree, sorted-neighbor-degrees) tuples. Use for quick isomorphism pruning. Test.
```falcon
func graphSignature(graph) = {
 
local sig = []
  for (node, neighbors in graph) {
 
local deg = neighbors.listLen()
 
local neighborDegs = []
    for (nb in neighbors) {
      neighborDegs.add(graph.get(nb, []).listLen())
    }
    neighborDegs.sort()
    sig.add([deg, neighborDegs])
  }
  sig.sort()
  sig
}
local g = {"A": ["B","C"], "B": ["A","C","D"], "C": ["A","B"], "D": ["B"]}
```

Write `matchingList(graph)` — greedy maximum matching as list of `[u,v]` pairs. Test.
```falcon
func matchingList(graph) = {
 
local matched = []
 
local pairs = []
  for (u, neighbors in graph) {
    if (!matched.containsItem(u)) {
      for (v in neighbors) {
        if (!matched.containsItem(v) && !matched.containsItem(u)) {
          pairs.add([u, v])
          matched.add(u)
          matched.add(v)
        }
      }
    }
  }
  pairs
}
local g = {"A": ["B","C"], "B": ["A","D"], "C": ["A"], "D": ["B"]}
println(matchingList(g))
```

Write a for-each loop over edges sorted by weight, building MST using union-find. Print each added edge.
```falcon
global ufParent = {}
func ufFind(x) = {
  if (this.ufParent.get(x, x) === x) {
    x
  } else {
 
local root = ufFind(this.ufParent.get(x, x))
    this.ufParent.set(x, root)
    root
  }
}
func ufUnion(x, y) {
 
local rx = ufFind(x)
 
local ry = ufFind(y)
  if (rx !== ry) {
    this.ufParent.set(rx, ry)
  }
}
local edges = [[1,"A","B"],[2,"B","C"],[3,"A","C"],[4,"C","D"]]
local nodes = ["A","B","C","D"]
for (n in nodes) { this.ufParent.set(n, n) }
local sortedEdges = edges.sort()
for (e in sortedEdges) {
 
local w = e[1]
 
local u = e[2]
 
local v = e[3]
  if (ufFind(u) !== ufFind(v)) {
    ufUnion(u, v)
    println("Added edge: " _ u _ "-" _ v _ " weight=" _ w)
  }
}
```

Write `graphKernel(graph, labels)` — Weisfeiler-Lehman graph kernel step: relabel each node by sorted neighbor labels. Return new label dict. Test.
```falcon
func graphKernel(graph, labels) = {
 
local newLabels = {}
  for (node, neighbors in graph) {
 
local nbLabels = []
    for (nb in neighbors) {
      nbLabels.add(labels.get(nb, ""))
    }
    nbLabels.sort()
 
local newLabel = labels.get(node, "") _ "|" _ nbLabels.join(",")
    newLabels.set(node, newLabel)
  }
  newLabels
}
local g = {"A":["B","C"],"B":["A"],"C":["A"]}
local labels = {"A":"1","B":"2","C":"2"}
println(graphKernel(g, labels))
```

Write `center(graph)` — nodes with eccentricity equal to radius. Test.
```falcon
func bfsEccentricity(graph, start) = {
 
local dist = {}
 
local queue = [start]
  dist.set(start, 0)
  while (queue.listLen() > 0) {
 
local cur = queue[1]
    queue.remove(1)
    for (nb in graph.get(cur, [])) {
      if (!dist.containsKey(nb)) {
        dist.set(nb, dist.get(cur, 0) + 1)
        queue.add(nb)
      }
    }
  }
 
local maxDist = 0
  for (n, d in dist) {
    if (d > maxDist) { maxDist = d }
  }
  maxDist
}
func center(graph) = {
 
local nodes = graph.keys()
 
local eccs = {}
  for (n in nodes) {
    eccs.set(n, bfsEccentricity(graph, n))
  }
 
local radius = 999999
  for (n, e in eccs) {
    if (e < radius) { radius = e }
  }
 
local result = []
  for (n, e in eccs) {
    if (e == radius) { result.add(n) }
  }
  result
}
local g = {"A":["B"],"B":["A","C","D"],"C":["B"],"D":["B","E"],"E":["D"]}
println(center(g))
```

Write `networkReliability(graph, p)` — estimate probability all nodes remain connected with each edge surviving with prob p. Monte Carlo 100 trials.
```falcon
func networkReliability(graph, p) = {
 
local nodes = graph.keys()
 
local n = nodes.listLen()
 
local edges = []
  for (u, neighbors in graph) {
    for (v in neighbors) {
      if (u << v) {
        edges.add([u, v])
      }
    }
  }
 
local connected = 0
  for (trial: 1 .. 100) {
 
local activeEdges = {}
    for (nd in nodes) { activeEdges.set(nd, []) }
    for (e in edges) {
      if (randFloat() < p) {
        activeEdges.get(e[1], []).add(e[2])
        activeEdges.get(e[2], []).add(e[1])
      }
    }
 
local visited = []
 
local queue = [nodes[1]]
    visited.add(nodes[1])
    while (queue.listLen() > 0) {
 
local cur = queue[1]
      queue.remove(1)
      for (nb in activeEdges.get(cur, [])) {
        if (!visited.containsItem(nb)) {
          visited.add(nb)
          queue.add(nb)
        }
      }
    }
    if (visited.listLen() == n) {
      connected = connected + 1
    }
  }
  connected / 100
}
local g = {"A":["B","C"],"B":["A","C"],"C":["A","B"]}
println(networkReliability(g, 0.9))
```

Write `powerGraph(graph, k)` — k-th power: add edge if path of length ≤ k. Test P5 squared.
```falcon
func powerGraph(graph, k) = {
 
local nodes = graph.keys()
 
local result = {}
  for (n in nodes) { result.set(n, []) }
  for (src in nodes) {
 
local dist = {}
    dist.set(src, 0)
 
local queue = [src]
    while (queue.listLen() > 0) {
 
local cur = queue[1]
      queue.remove(1)
      for (nb in graph.get(cur, [])) {
        if (!dist.containsKey(nb)) {
          dist.set(nb, dist.get(cur, 0) + 1)
          queue.add(nb)
        }
      }
    }
    for (nd, d in dist) {
      if (d > 0 && d <= k && !result.get(src, []).containsItem(nd)) {
        result.get(src, []).add(nd)
      }
    }
  }
  result
}
local p5 = {"1":["2"],"2":["1","3"],"3":["2","4"],"4":["3","5"],"5":["4"]}
println(powerGraph(p5, 2))
```

Write `shortestPathStr(graph, u, v)` — run Dijkstra and format result as `"u→...→v (cost: C)"`. Test.
```falcon
func dijkstra(graph, start) = {
 
local dist = {}
 
local prev = {}
 
local visited = []
  for (n, edges in graph) {
    dist.set(n, 999999)
  }
  dist.set(start, 0)
 
local queue = [start]
  while (queue.listLen() > 0) {
 
local best = 999999
 
local cur = queue[1]
    for (n in queue) {
      if (dist.get(n, 999999) < best) {
        best = dist.get(n, 999999)
        cur = n
      }
    }
    queue.remove(queue.indexOf(cur))
    visited.add(cur)
    for (nb, w in graph.get(cur, {})) {
 
local newDist = dist.get(cur, 999999) + w
      if (newDist < dist.get(nb, 999999)) {
        dist.set(nb, newDist)
        prev.set(nb, cur)
        if (!visited.containsItem(nb) && !queue.containsItem(nb)) {
          queue.add(nb)
        }
      }
    }
  }
 
local listResult = [dist, prev]
  listResult
}
func shortestPathStr(graph, u, v) = {
 
local dp = dijkstra(graph, u)
 
local dist = dp[1]
 
local prev = dp[2]
 
local path = []
 
local cur = v
  while (cur !== u) {
    path.add(cur)
    cur = prev.get(cur, u)
  }
  path.add(u)
  path.reverseList()
  path.join("→") _ " (cost: " _ dist.get(v, 999999) _ ")"
}
local wg = {"A":{"B":1,"C":4},"B":{"A":1,"C":2,"D":5},"C":{"A":4,"B":2,"D":1},"D":{"C":1,"B":5}}
println(shortestPathStr(wg, "A", "D"))
```

Write `subtreeNodes(tree, root, node)` — all nodes in subtree rooted at `node`. Return list. Test.
```falcon
func subtreeNodes(tree, root, node) = {
 
local result = []
 
local stack = [node]
  while (stack.listLen() > 0) {
 
local cur = stack[stack.listLen()]
    stack.remove(stack.listLen())
    result.add(cur)
    for (child in tree.get(cur, [])) {
      stack.add(child)
    }
  }
  result
}
local t = {"A":["B","C"],"B":["D","E"],"C":["F"],"D":[],"E":[],"F":[]}
```

```falcon
func distDict(graph, source) = {
 
local dist = {}
 
local queue = [source]
  dist.set(source, 0)
  while (queue.listLen() > 0) {
 
local cur = queue[1]
    queue.remove(1)
    for (nb in graph.get(cur, [])) {
      if (!dist.containsKey(nb)) {
        dist.set(nb, dist.get(cur, 0) + 1)
        queue.add(nb)
      }
    }
  }
  dist
}
```

Write `bipartiteDict(graph)` — `{node: side}` for bipartite check. Return `"NOT_BIPARTITE"` if fails. Test.
```falcon
func bipartiteDict(graph) = {
 
local color = {}
 
local nodes = graph.keys()
 
local isBipartite = true
  for (start in nodes) {
    if (!color.containsKey(start) && isBipartite) {
 
local queue = [start]
      color.set(start, 0)
      while (queue.listLen() > 0 && isBipartite) {
 
local cur = queue[1]
        queue.remove(1)
        for (nb in graph.get(cur, [])) {
          if (!color.containsKey(nb)) {
            color.set(nb, 1 - color.get(cur, 0))
            queue.add(nb)
          } else if (color.get(nb, 0) == color.get(cur, 0)) {
            isBipartite = false
          }
        }
      }
    }
  }
  if (isBipartite) { color } else { "NOT_BIPARTITE" }
}
local bg = {"A":["B","D"],"B":["A","C"],"C":["B","D"],"D":["A","C"]}
println(bipartiteDict(bg))
local ng = {"A":["B","C"],"B":["A","C"],"C":["A","B"]}
println(bipartiteDict(ng))
```

Write `orientationDict(graph)` — acyclic orientation: assign direction to each undirected edge. Return edge direction dict. Test.
```falcon
func orientationDict(graph) = {
 
local oriented = {}
 
local visited = []
 
local nodes = graph.keys()
  for (start in nodes) {
    if (!visited.containsItem(start)) {
 
local queue = [start]
      visited.add(start)
      while (queue.listLen() > 0) {
 
local cur = queue[1]
        queue.remove(1)
        for (nb in graph.get(cur, [])) {
 
local key = cur _ "->" _ nb
 
local rkey = nb _ "->" _ cur
          if (!oriented.containsKey(key) && !oriented.containsKey(rkey)) {
            oriented.set(key, [cur, nb])
          }
          if (!visited.containsItem(nb)) {
            visited.add(nb)
            queue.add(nb)
          }
        }
      }
    }
  }
  oriented
}
local g = {"A":["B","C"],"B":["A","C"],"C":["A","B"]}
println(orientationDict(g))
```

Write `weightedEdgePalette(weights)` — normalize weights and assign colors. Return edge → color dict. Test.
```falcon
func weightedEdgePalette(weights) = {
 
local minW = 999999
 
local maxW = -999999
  for (e, w in weights) {
    if (w < minW) { minW = w }
    if (w > maxW) { maxW = w }
  }
 
local palette = {}
  for (e, w in weights) {
 
local t = if (maxW == minW) 0 else (w - minW) / (maxW - minW)
 
local r = round(t * 255)
 
local g = round((1 - t) * 255)
    palette.set(e, makeColor([r, g, 0]))
  }
  palette
}
local weights = {"A-B": 1, "B-C": 5, "C-D": 3, "A-D": 8}
```

Write a for-each loop over edges to build the line graph (edges become nodes connected if they share an endpoint).
```falcon
local edges = [["A","B"],["B","C"],["A","C"],["C","D"]]
local lineGraph = {}
for (i: 1 .. edges.listLen()) {
 
local key = edges[i][1] _ "-" _ edges[i][2]
  lineGraph.set(key, [])
}
for (i: 1 .. edges.listLen()) {
  for (j: 1 .. edges.listLen()) {
    if (i != j) {
 
local ei = edges[i]
 
local ej = edges[j]
 
local ki = ei[1] _ "-" _ ei[2]
 
local kj = ej[1] _ "-" _ ej[2]
      if (ei[1] === ej[1] || ei[1] === ej[2] || ei[2] === ej[1] || ei[2] === ej[2]) {
        if (!lineGraph.get(ki, []).containsItem(kj)) {
          lineGraph.get(ki, []).add(kj)
        }
      }
    }
  }
}
println(lineGraph)
```

Write `graphFeedback(graph)` — feedback arc set approximation for DAG. Return edges to remove. Test.
```falcon
func graphFeedback(graph) = {
 
local removed = []
 
local remaining = copyDict(graph)
 
local changed = true
  while (changed) {
    changed = false
 
local nodes = remaining.keys()
    for (n in nodes) {
 
local inDeg = 0
      for (u, neighbors in remaining) {
        if (neighbors.containsItem(n)) {
          inDeg = inDeg + 1
        }
      }
      if (inDeg == 0 || remaining.get(n, []).listLen() == 0) {
        remaining.delete(n)
        changed = true
      }
    }
  }
  for (u, neighbors in remaining) {
    for (v in neighbors) {
      removed.add([u, v])
    }
  }
  removed
}
local dag = {"A":["B","C"],"B":["D"],"C":["B"],"D":["A"],"E":["D"]}
println(graphFeedback(dag))
```

Write `parallelBFS(graph, sources)` — multi-source BFS. Return shortest distance from any source. Test.
```falcon
func parallelBFS(graph, sources) = {
 
local dist = {}
 
local queue = []
  for (s in sources) {
    dist.set(s, 0)
    queue.add(s)
  }
  while (queue.listLen() > 0) {
 
local cur = queue[1]
    queue.remove(1)
    for (nb in graph.get(cur, [])) {
      if (!dist.containsKey(nb)) {
        dist.set(nb, dist.get(cur, 0) + 1)
        queue.add(nb)
      }
    }
  }
  dist
}
local g = {"A":["B","C"],"B":["A","D"],"C":["A","E"],"D":["B"],"E":["C","F"],"F":["E"]}
println(parallelBFS(g, ["A","F"]))
```

Write `dominatorTree(graph, source)` — compute dominator tree (simplified: for each node, remove it and BFS). Return dominator dict. Test.
```falcon
func dominatorTree(graph, source) = {
 
local nodes = graph.keys()
 
local dominators = {}
  for (n in nodes) {
    if (n === source) {
      dominators.set(n, source)
    } else {
 
local tempGraph = copyDict(graph)
      tempGraph.delete(n)
 
local reachable = []
 
local queue = [source]
      while (queue.listLen() > 0) {
 
local cur = queue[1]
        queue.remove(1)
        if (!reachable.containsItem(cur)) {
          reachable.add(cur)
          for (nb in tempGraph.get(cur, [])) {
            if (!reachable.containsItem(nb)) {
              queue.add(nb)
            }
          }
        }
      }
      if (!reachable.containsItem(n)) {
        dominators.set(n, source)
      } else {
        dominators.set(n, "none")
      }
    }
  }
  dominators
}
local g = {"start":["A","B"],"A":["C"],"B":["C"],"C":["end"],"end":[]}
println(dominatorTree(g, "start"))
```

```falcon
local nodes6 = ["A","B","C","D","E","F"]
local graph6 = {"A":["B","C","D"],"B":["A","C","E"],"C":["A","B","F"],"D":["A","E"],"E":["B","D","F"],"F":["C","E"]}
local cliques3 = []
for (i: 1 .. 4) {
  for (j: i+1 .. 5) {
    for (k: j+1 .. 6) {
 
local u = nodes6[i]
 
local v = nodes6[j]
 
local w = nodes6[k]
      if (graph6.get(u,[]).containsItem(v) && graph6.get(v,[]).containsItem(w) && graph6.get(u,[]).containsItem(w)) {
        cliques3.add([u,v,w])
      }
    }
  }
}
```

Write a for loop generating the De Bruijn sequence for alphabet size 2 and order 3 by following the De Bruijn graph.
```falcon
local db = []
local dbState = [0,0,0]
local dbVisited = []
local dbSteps = 0
while (dbSteps < 8) {
 
local key = dbState.join("")
  if (!dbVisited.containsItem(key)) {
    dbVisited.add(key)
  }
 
local newBit = (dbState[1] + dbState[2]) % 2
  db.add(dbState[3])
  dbState = [newBit, dbState[1], dbState[2]]
  dbSteps = dbSteps + 1
}
println(db)
```

Write a while loop implementing iterative deepening DFS. Print each depth limit and nodes visited.
```falcon
local iiddfsTarget = "G"
local iiddfsGraph = {"A":["B","C"],"B":["D","E"],"C":["F","G"],"D":[],"E":[],"F":[],"G":[]}
local iiddfsFound = false
local iiddfsDepth = 0
while (!iiddfsFound && iiddfsDepth <= 10) {
  println("Depth limit: " _ iiddfsDepth)
 
local visited = []
 
local stack = [["A", 0]]
  while (stack.listLen() > 0 && !iiddfsFound) {
 
local cur = stack[stack.listLen()]
    stack.remove(stack.listLen())
 
local node = cur[1]
 
local depth = cur[2]
    if (!visited.containsItem(node _ "," _ depth)) {
      visited.add(node _ "," _ depth)
      println("  Visited: " _ node)
      if (node === iiddfsTarget) {
        iiddfsFound = true
        println("Found at depth " _ depth)
      } else if (depth < iiddfsDepth) {
        for (nb in iiddfsGraph.get(node, [])) {
          stack.add([nb, depth + 1])
        }
      }
    }
  }
  iiddfsDepth = iiddfsDepth + 1
}
```

Write a while loop simulating diffusion on a graph: each tick, each node's value spreads to neighbors. Run 5 ticks.
```falcon
local diffGraph = {"A":["B","C"],"B":["A","C"],"C":["A","B","D"],"D":["C"]}
local diffVals = {"A":100,"B":0,"C":0,"D":0}
for (tick: 1 .. 5) {
 
local newVals = {}
  for (n, neighbors in diffGraph) {
 
local sum = diffVals.get(n, 0)
    for (nb in neighbors) {
      sum = sum + diffVals.get(nb, 0)
    }
    newVals.set(n, sum / (neighbors.listLen() + 1))
  }
  diffVals = newVals
  println("Tick " _ tick _ ": " _ diffVals)
}
```

Write `randic(graph)` — sum of 1/sqrt(d_u * d_v) over all edges. Test.
```falcon
func randic(graph) = {
 
local sum = 0
  for (u, neighbors in graph) {
 
local du = neighbors.listLen()
    for (v in neighbors) {
      if (u << v) {
 
local dv = graph.get(v, []).listLen()
        sum = sum + 1 / sqrt(du * dv)
      }
    }
  }
  sum
}
local g = {"A":["B","C"],"B":["A","C"],"C":["A","B"]}
println(randic(g))
```

Write `tsp2Opt(tour, dist_matrix)` — 2-opt improvement on an initial TSP tour. Apply one pass.
```falcon
func tsp2Opt(tour, dist_matrix) = {
 
local improved = copyList(tour)
 
local n = tour.listLen() - 1
  for (i: 1 .. n - 1) {
    for (j: i+2 .. n) {
 
local d1 = dist_matrix[improved[i]][improved[i+1]] + dist_matrix[improved[j]][improved[j+1]]
 
local d2 = dist_matrix[improved[i]][improved[j]] + dist_matrix[improved[i+1]][improved[j+1]]
      if (d2 < d1) {
 
local seg = []
        for (k: i+1 .. j) { seg.add(improved[k]) }
        seg.reverseList()
        for (k: i+1 .. j) { improved[k] = seg[k-i] }
      }
    }
  }
  improved
}
local dm = [[0,10,15,20],[10,0,35,25],[15,35,0,30],[20,25,30,0]]
println(tsp2Opt([1,2,3,4,1], dm))
```

Write `floydTrace(dist, i, j, k)` — print when a shorter path is found: `"dist[i][j] updated via k"`. Test.
```falcon
func floydTrace(dist, n) {
  for (k: 1 .. n) {
    for (i: 1 .. n) {
      for (j: 1 .. n) {
        if (dist[i][k] + dist[k][j] < dist[i][j]) {
          println("dist[" _ i _ "][" _ j _ "] updated via " _ k)
          dist[i][j] = dist[i][k] + dist[k][j]
        }
      }
    }
  }
}
local d = [[0,999,3,999],[2,0,999,999],[999,7,0,1],[6,999,999,0]]
floydTrace(d, 4)
```

```falcon
local row = labels[i] _ " [ "
```

Write `flowDict(network, source, sink)` — run max-flow, return flow values as nested dict. Test.
```falcon
func flowDict(network, source, sink) = {
 
local flow = {}
  for (u, edges in network) {
    flow.set(u, {})
    for (v, cap in edges) {
      flow.get(u, {}).set(v, 0)
    }
  }
 
local totalFlow = 0
 
local running = true
  while (running) {
 
local parent = {}
 
local visited = [source]
 
local queue = [source]
    while (queue.listLen() > 0) {
 
local cur = queue[1]
      queue.remove(1)
      for (v, cap in network.get(cur, {})) {
 
local f = flow.get(cur, {}).get(v, 0)
        if (!visited.containsItem(v) && cap - f > 0) {
          parent.set(v, cur)
          visited.add(v)
          queue.add(v)
        }
      }
    }
    if (!visited.containsItem(sink)) {
      running = false
    } else {
 
local pathFlow = 999999
 
local cur = sink
      while (cur !== source) {
 
local prev = parent.get(cur, "")
 
local cap = network.get(prev, {}).get(cur, 0)
 
local f = flow.get(prev, {}).get(cur, 0)
        if (cap - f < pathFlow) { pathFlow = cap - f }
        cur = prev
      }
      cur = sink
      while (cur !== source) {
 
local prev = parent.get(cur, "")
        flow.get(prev, {}).set(cur, flow.get(prev, {}).get(cur, 0) + pathFlow)
        flow.get(cur, {}).set(prev, flow.get(cur, {}).get(prev, 0) - pathFlow)
        cur = prev
      }
      totalFlow = totalFlow + pathFlow
    }
  }
  flow
}
local net = {"S":{"A":10,"B":10},"A":{"T":10},"B":{"T":10},"T":{}}
println(flowDict(net, "S", "T"))
```

```falcon
func flowColor(utilization) = {
  if (utilization <= 0.5) {
 
local t = utilization * 2
 
local r = round(t * 255)
    makeColor([r, 255, 0])
  } else {
 
local t = (utilization - 0.5) * 2
 
local g = round((1 - t) * 255)
    makeColor([255, g, 0])
  }
}
println(flowColor(0))
```

Write `avgClusteringCoeff(graph)` — average clustering coefficient across all nodes. Test.
```falcon
func clusteringCoeff(graph, node) = {
 
local neighbors = graph.get(node, [])
 
local k = neighbors.listLen()
  if (k < 2) {
    0
  } else {
 
local links = 0
    for (i: 1 .. k) {
      for (j: i+1 .. k) {
        if (graph.get(neighbors[i], []).containsItem(neighbors[j])) {
          links = links + 1
        }
      }
    }
    2 * links / (k * (k - 1))
  }
}
func avgClusteringCoeff(graph) = {
 
local nodes = graph.keys()
 
local total = 0
  for (n in nodes) {
    total = total + clusteringCoeff(graph, n)
  }
  total / nodes.listLen()
}
local g = {"A":["B","C","D"],"B":["A","C"],"C":["A","B","D"],"D":["A","C"]}
println(avgClusteringCoeff(g))
```

Write `anagramGroups(words)` that takes a list of words and groups anagrams together. Sort each word's characters to form a key, then group words with the same key. Return a list of groups (each group is a list).
```falcon
func anagramGroups(words) = {
 
local groups = {}
  for (word in words) {
 
local chars = word.split("")
    chars.sort()
 
local key = chars.join("")
    if (!groups.containsKey(key)) {
      groups.set(key, [])
    }
    groups.get(key, []).add(word)
  }
  groups.values()
}
println(anagramGroups(["eat","tea","tan","ate","nat","bat"]))
```

Implement `substringSearch(text, patterns)` where `patterns` is a list. Return a dict mapping each pattern to its list of start positions (1-based) in `text`. Handle multiple patterns efficiently.
```falcon
func substringSearch(text, patterns) = {
 
local result = {}
  for (pattern in patterns) {
 
local positions = []
 
local plen = pattern.textLen()
 
local tlen = text.textLen()
    for (i: 1 .. tlen - plen + 1) {
      if (text.segment(i, plen) === pattern) {
        positions.add(i)
      }
    }
    result.set(pattern, positions)
  }
  result
}
println(substringSearch("abcabcabc", ["abc","ab","bc"]))
```

Write `inverseBWT(transformed)` that inverts the Burrows-Wheeler Transform by repeatedly sorting and using the first/last column relationship to reconstruct the original string.
```falcon
func inverseBWT(transformed) = {
 
local n = transformed.textLen()
 
local table = []
  for (i: 1 .. n) { table.add("") }
  for (iter: 1 .. n) {
    for (i: 1 .. n) {
      table[i] = transformed.segment(i, 1) _ table[i]
    }
    table.sort()
  }
 
local result = ""
  for (row in table) {
    if (row.segment(row.textLen(), 1) === "$") {
      result = row
    }
  }
  result
}
println(inverseBWT("BNN$AA"))
```

Implement `kmpSearchList(text_chars, pattern_chars)` performing KMP search on lists of characters. Return all 1-based starting positions of matches.
```falcon
func kmpSearchList(text_chars, pattern_chars) = {
 
local m = pattern_chars.listLen()
 
local n = text_chars.listLen()
 
local fail = []
  for (i: 1 .. m) { fail.add(0) }
 
local k = 0
  for (i: 2 .. m) {
    while (k > 0 && pattern_chars[k+1] !== pattern_chars[i]) {
      k = fail[k]
    }
    if (pattern_chars[k+1] === pattern_chars[i]) { k = k + 1 }
    fail[i] = k
  }
 
local matches = []
  k = 0
  for (i: 1 .. n) {
    while (k > 0 && pattern_chars[k+1] !== text_chars[i]) {
      k = fail[k]
    }
    if (pattern_chars[k+1] === text_chars[i]) { k = k + 1 }
    if (k == m) {
      matches.add(i - m + 1)
      k = fail[k]
    }
  }
  matches
}
println(kmpSearchList("abcabcabc".split(""), "abc".split("")))
```

Implement `topNEntries(freqDict, n)` returning a list of the top `n` `[key, count]` pairs from `freqDict`, sorted by count descending, then by key alphabetically for ties.
```falcon
func topNEntries(freqDict, n) = {
 
local pairs = []
  for (k, v in freqDict) {
    pairs.add([k, v])
  }
  pairs.sort { a, b ->
    if (a[2] != b[2]) a[2] > b[2] else a[1] << b[1]
  }
  pairs.slice(1, n)
}
println(topNEntries({"apple":5,"banana":3,"cherry":5,"date":1}, 2))
```

Write `buildKeyPhraseDict(text, stopWords)` extracting key phrases: 2–3 word sequences where no word is a stop word, counting frequency. Return dict of phrase→count, sorted by count.
```falcon
func buildKeyPhraseDict(text, stopWords) = {
 
local words = text.lowercase().splitAtSpaces()
 
local phrases = {}
  for (i: 1 .. words.listLen() - 1) {
    if (!stopWords.containsItem(words[i]) && !stopWords.containsItem(words[i+1])) {
 
local phrase = words[i] _ " " _ words[i+1]
      phrases.set(phrase, phrases.get(phrase, 0) + 1)
    }
    if (i <= words.listLen() - 2 && !stopWords.containsItem(words[i]) && !stopWords.containsItem(words[i+1]) && !stopWords.containsItem(words[i+2])) {
 
local phrase3 = words[i] _ " " _ words[i+1] _ " " _ words[i+2]
      phrases.set(phrase3, phrases.get(phrase3, 0) + 1)
    }
  }
  phrases
}
println(buildKeyPhraseDict("the quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog", ["the","over"]))
```

Write a program using `while` to implement a two-pointer approach finding all pairs of words in `["a","bb","ccc","dd","e"]` whose combined length equals 4. Track `left` and `right` pointers on the length-sorted list.
```falcon
local wlist = ["a","bb","ccc","dd","e"]
local lens = wlist.map { w -> w.textLen() }
local sorted = copyList(wlist)
sorted.sort { a, b -> a.textLen() < b.textLen() }
local left = 1
local right = sorted.listLen()
while (left < right) {
 
local combined = sorted[left].textLen() + sorted[right].textLen()
  if (combined == 4) {
    println(sorted[left] _ " + " _ sorted[right])
    left = left + 1
    right = right - 1
  } else if (combined < 4) {
    left = left + 1
  } else {
    right = right - 1
  }
}
```

Implement `buildSubstitutionKey(key)` generating a substitution cipher key: given a keyword like `"KEYWORD"`, build a dict mapping each plaintext letter to a ciphertext letter using the keyword-based alphabet.
```falcon
func buildSubstitutionKey(key) = {
 
local alpha = "ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZ"
 
local seen = []
 
local keyAlpha = ""
 
local kup = key.uppercase()
  for (i: 1 .. kup.textLen()) {
 
local ch = kup.segment(i, 1)
    if (!seen.containsItem(ch) && alpha.contains(ch)) {
      seen.add(ch)
      keyAlpha = keyAlpha _ ch
    }
  }
  for (i: 1 .. 26) {
 
local ch = alpha.segment(i, 1)
    if (!seen.containsItem(ch)) {
      keyAlpha = keyAlpha _ ch
    }
  }
 
local dict = {}
  for (i: 1 .. 26) {
    dict.set(alpha.segment(i, 1), keyAlpha.segment(i, 1))
  }
  dict
}
println(buildSubstitutionKey("KEYWORD"))
```

Implement `generateFromMarkov(matrix, start, length)` using the normalized transition matrix. At each step, choose the most probable next character (argmax), building a string of `length` characters starting from `start`.
```falcon
func generateFromMarkov(matrix, start, length) = {
 
local result = start
 
local cur = start
  for (i: 2 .. length) {
 
local row = matrix.get(cur, {})
 
local bestProb = -1
 
local nextChar = cur
    for (ch, prob in row) {
      if (prob > bestProb) {
        bestProb = prob
        nextChar = ch
      }
    }
    result = result _ nextChar
    cur = nextChar
  }
  result
}
local mm = {"a":{"b":0.7,"c":0.3},"b":{"a":0.4,"c":0.6},"c":{"a":0.9,"b":0.1}}
println(generateFromMarkov(mm, "a", 8))
```

Implement `dfaMatch(dfa, text)` that runs `text` through the DFA dict and returns `true` if the text is accepted (reaches the accept state), `false` otherwise.
```falcon
func dfaMatch(dfa, text) = {
 
local state = dfa.get("start", 0)
  for (i: 1 .. text.textLen()) {
 
local ch = text.segment(i, 1)
 
local transitions = dfa.get("transitions", {}).get(state, {})
    state = transitions.get(ch, -1)
    if (state == -1) {
      state = -1
      i = text.textLen() + 1
    }
  }
 
local accepts = dfa.get("accept", [])
  accepts.containsItem(state)
}
local dfa = {"start": 0, "accept": [2], "transitions": {0: {"a": 1}, 1: {"b": 2}, 2: {}}}
println(dfaMatch(dfa, "ab"))
println(dfaMatch(dfa, "abc"))
```

Implement `func longestPalindrome(s) = { ... }` using Manacher's algorithm to return the longest palindromic substring in O(n). Use the transformed string approach with `"#"` separators.
```falcon
func longestPalindrome(s) = {
 
local t = "#"
  for (i: 1 .. s.textLen()) {
    t = t _ s.segment(i, 1) _ "#"
  }
 
local n = t.textLen()
 
local p = []
  for (i: 1 .. n) { p.add(0) }
 
local c = 1
 
local r = 1
  for (i: 2 .. n) {
 
local mirror = 2 * c - i
    if (i < r && mirror >= 1) {
      p[i] = if (r - i < p[mirror]) r - i else p[mirror]
    }
 
local a = i - (p[i] + 1)
 
local b = i + (p[i] + 1)
    while (a >= 1 && b <= n && t.segment(a, 1) === t.segment(b, 1)) {
      p[i] = p[i] + 1
      a = a - 1
      b = b + 1
    }
    if (i + p[i] > r) {
      c = i
      r = i + p[i]
    }
  }
 
local maxLen = 0
 
local center = 1
  for (i: 1 .. n) {
    if (p[i] > maxLen) {
      maxLen = p[i]
      center = i
    }
  }
 
local start = floor((center - maxLen) / 2) + 1
  s.segment(start, maxLen)
}
println(longestPalindrome("babad"))
println(longestPalindrome("cbbd"))
println(longestPalindrome("racecar"))
```

Write `func jaroWinkler(s1, s2) = { ... }` computing the Jaro-Winkler similarity as a float in `[0,1]`. Then write `func findMostSimilar(word, candidates) = { ... }` returning the candidate with highest Jaro-Winkler score.
```falcon
func jaroWinkler(s1, s2) = {
 
local l1 = s1.textLen()
 
local l2 = s2.textLen()
  if (l1 == 0 && l2 == 0) {
    1
  } else {
 
local matchWindow = floor(if (l1 > l2) l1 else l2) / 2 - 1
 
local s1matched = []
 
local s2matched = []
    for (i: 1 .. l1) { s1matched.add(false) }
    for (i: 1 .. l2) { s2matched.add(false) }
 
local matches = 0
    for (i: 1 .. l1) {
 
local start = if (i - matchWindow > 1) round(i - matchWindow) else 1
 
local end = if (i + matchWindow < l2) round(i + matchWindow) else l2
      for (j: start .. end) {
        if (!s2matched[j] && s1.segment(i,1) === s2.segment(j,1)) {
          s1matched[i] = true
          s2matched[j] = true
          matches = matches + 1
          j = end + 1
        }
      }
    }
    if (matches == 0) {
      0
    } else {
 
local trans = 0
 
local k = 1
      for (i: 1 .. l1) {
        if (s1matched[i]) {
          while (!s2matched[k]) { k = k + 1 }
          if (s1.segment(i,1) !== s2.segment(k,1)) { trans = trans + 1 }
          k = k + 1
        }
      }
 
local jaro = (matches/l1 + matches/l2 + (matches - trans/2)/matches) / 3
 
local prefix = 0
      for (i: 1 .. 4) {
        if (i <= l1 && i <= l2 && s1.segment(i,1) === s2.segment(i,1)) {
          prefix = prefix + 1
        }
      }
      jaro + prefix * 0.1 * (1 - jaro)
    }
  }
}
func findMostSimilar(word, candidates) = {
 
local best = -1
 
local bestWord = ""
  for (c in candidates) {
 
local score = jaroWinkler(word, c)
    if (score > best) {
      best = score
      bestWord = c
    }
  }
  bestWord
}
println(jaroWinkler("MARTHA", "MARHTA"))
println(findMostSimilar("helo", ["hello","world","help","hero"]))
```

Write `func decompressString(s) = { ... }` for a nested run-length encoding like `"3[a2[b]]"`. Use a stack: push counts and partial results on `[`, pop and repeat on `]`. Return the fully expanded string.
```falcon
func decompressString(s) = {
 
local stack = []
 
local currentStr = ""
 
local currentNum = 0
  for (i: 1 .. s.textLen()) {
 
local ch = s.segment(i, 1)
    if (ch ? number) {
      currentNum = currentNum * 10 + ch
    } else if (ch === "[") {
      stack.add([currentNum, currentStr])
      currentStr = ""
      currentNum = 0
    } else if (ch === "]") {
 
local top = stack[stack.listLen()]
      stack.remove(stack.listLen())
 
local times = top[1]
 
local prev = top[2]
 
local repeated = ""
      for (k: 1 .. times) { repeated = repeated _ currentStr }
      currentStr = prev _ repeated
    } else {
      currentStr = currentStr _ ch
    }
  }
  currentStr
}
println(decompressString("3[a2[b]]"))
println(decompressString("2[abc]3[d]"))
```

Write `func detectAnomalousWords(text, languageModel) = { ... }` identifying words whose bigram probability given context is below a threshold. Return a list of `{"word":w,"position":i,"prob":p}` dicts.
```falcon
func detectAnomalousWords(text, languageModel) = {
 
local words = text.splitAtSpaces()
 
local result = []
 
local threshold = 0.01
  for (i: 2 .. words.listLen()) {
 
local prev = words[i-1]
 
local word = words[i]
 
local bigrams = languageModel.get("bigrams", {})
 
local unigrams = languageModel.get("unigrams", {})
 
local total = languageModel.get("total", 1)
 
local pPrev = unigrams.get(prev, 0) / total
 
local pWord = unigrams.get(word, 0) / total
 
local pBigram = bigrams.get(prev _ " " _ word, 0) / total
 
local prob = if (pPrev > 0) pBigram / pPrev else 0
    if (prob < threshold) {
      result.add({"word": word, "position": i, "prob": prob})
    }
  }
  result
}
local lm = {"unigrams":{"the":10,"cat":5,"sat":4,"on":3},"bigrams":{"the cat":4,"cat sat":3},"total":50}
println(detectAnomalousWords("the cat sat on mars", lm))
```

Implement a program with nested `while` loops computing the Aho-Corasick failure links for patterns `["he","she","his","hers"]`. Build the trie, then BFS to compute failure and output links.
```falcon
local acPatterns = ["he","she","his","hers"]
local acTrie = {"root":{}}
for (pat in acPatterns) {
 
local node = acTrie.get("root", {})
  for (i: 1 .. pat.textLen()) {
 
local ch = pat.segment(i, 1)
    if (!node.containsKey(ch)) { node.set(ch, {}) }
    node = node.get(ch, {})
  }
  node.set("$", pat)
}
local failLinks = {}
local bfsQueue = []
for (key, val in acTrie.get("root", {})) {
  if (key !== "$") {
    failLinks.set(key, "root")
    bfsQueue.add([key, val])
  }
}
local qi = 1
while (qi <= bfsQueue.listLen()) {
 
local cur = bfsQueue[qi]
 
local curKey = cur[1]
 
local curNode = cur[2]
  for (ch, child in curNode) {
    if (ch !== "$") {
 
local fail = failLinks.get(curKey, "root")
 
local failNode = acTrie.get("root", {})
      if (failNode.containsKey(ch)) {
        failLinks.set(curKey _ ch, ch)
      } else {
        failLinks.set(curKey _ ch, "root")
      }
      bfsQueue.add([curKey _ ch, child])
    }
  }
  qi = qi + 1
}
println(failLinks)
```

Write `jaccardSimilarityLists(list1, list2)` computing Jaccard similarity between two lists treated as sets: `|intersection| / |union|`. Use `.containsItem()` to build intersection and union sets.
```falcon
func jaccardSimilarityLists(list1, list2) = {
 
local intersection = []
 
local union = copyList(list1)
  for (item in list1) {
    if (list2.containsItem(item)) {
      intersection.add(item)
    }
  }
  for (item in list2) {
    if (!union.containsItem(item)) {
      union.add(item)
    }
  }
  if (union.listLen() == 0) { 0 } else { intersection.listLen() / union.listLen() }
}
println(jaccardSimilarityLists(["a","b","c","d"],["b","c","d","e"]))
```

Write `buildPointwiseMutualInfo(wordList)` computing PMI for all bigrams: `PMI(a,b) = log(P(a,b) / (P(a)*P(b)))` where probabilities come from unigram and bigram counts. Return a dict of dicts.
```falcon
func buildPointwiseMutualInfo(wordList) = {
 
local n = wordList.listLen()
 
local unigrams = {}
 
local bigrams = {}
  for (w in wordList) {
    unigrams.set(w, unigrams.get(w, 0) + 1)
  }
  for (i: 1 .. n - 1) {
 
local bg = wordList[i] _ " " _ wordList[i+1]
    bigrams.set(bg, bigrams.get(bg, 0) + 1)
  }
 
local pmi = {}
  for (bg, cnt in bigrams) {
 
local parts = bg.splitAtFirst(" ")
 
local a = parts[1]
 
local b = parts[2]
 
local pab = cnt / n
 
local pa = unigrams.get(a, 1) / n
 
local pb = unigrams.get(b, 1) / n
    if (!pmi.containsKey(a)) { pmi.set(a, {}) }
    pmi.get(a, {}).set(b, log(pab / (pa * pb)))
  }
  pmi
}
println(buildPointwiseMutualInfo(["the","cat","sat","on","the","cat","ate","the","rat"]))
```

Implement `buildLanguageModelDict(corpus)` combining unigram, bigram, and trigram frequency dicts into a single nested dict structure: `{"unigrams":u, "bigrams":b, "trigrams":t, "vocabSize":n}`.
```falcon
func buildLanguageModelDict(corpus) = {
 
local words = corpus.splitAtSpaces()
 
local unigrams = {}
 
local bigrams = {}
 
local trigrams = {}
 
local vocab = []
  for (w in words) {
    unigrams.set(w, unigrams.get(w, 0) + 1)
    if (!vocab.containsItem(w)) { vocab.add(w) }
  }
  for (i: 1 .. words.listLen() - 1) {
 
local bg = words[i] _ " " _ words[i+1]
    bigrams.set(bg, bigrams.get(bg, 0) + 1)
  }
  for (i: 1 .. words.listLen() - 2) {
 
local tg = words[i] _ " " _ words[i+1] _ " " _ words[i+2]
    trigrams.set(tg, trigrams.get(tg, 0) + 1)
  }
  {"unigrams": unigrams, "bigrams": bigrams, "trigrams": trigrams, "vocabSize": vocab.listLen()}
}
println(buildLanguageModelDict("the cat sat on the mat the cat ate"))
```

Implement string similarity using cosine similarity on character bigram vectors: compute bigrams for `"hello"` and `"helo"`, build frequency vectors, and compute cosine similarity. Print with 4 decimal places.
```falcon
local h1 = "hello"
local h2 = "helo"
func getBigrams(s) = {
 
local bg = {}
  for (i: 1 .. s.textLen() - 1) {
 
local gram = s.segment(i,2)
    bg.set(gram, bg.get(gram, 0) + 1)
  }
  bg
}
local bg1 = getBigrams(h1)
local bg2 = getBigrams(h2)
local allKeys = []
for (k, v in bg1) { if (!allKeys.containsItem(k)) { allKeys.add(k) } }
for (k, v in bg2) { if (!allKeys.containsItem(k)) { allKeys.add(k) } }
local dot = 0
local mag1 = 0
local mag2 = 0
for (k in allKeys) {
 
local v1 = bg1.get(k, 0)
 
local v2 = bg2.get(k, 0)
  dot = dot + v1 * v2
  mag1 = mag1 + v1 * v1
  mag2 = mag2 + v2 * v2
}
local cosim = if (mag1 > 0 && mag2 > 0) dot / (sqrt(mag1) * sqrt(mag2)) else 0
println(formatDecimal(cosim, 4))
```

Write `countOccurrences(text, pattern)` returning the number of times `pattern` appears in `text` as a substring (non-overlapping). Test with `text = "abababab"`, `pattern = "aba"` expecting `2`.

```falcon
func countOccurrences(text, pattern) = {
```

Implement `zigzagEncode(s, rows)` that encodes string `s` in a zigzag pattern across `rows` rows. For `s = "PAYPALISHIRING"` and `rows = 3`, return `"PAHNAPLSIIGYIR"`.
```falcon
func zigzagEncode(s, rows) = {
 
local rowStrs = []
  for (i: 1 .. rows) { rowStrs.add("") }
 
local row = 1
 
local dir = 1
  for (i: 1 .. s.textLen()) {
    rowStrs[row] = rowStrs[row] _ s.segment(i,1)
    if (row == rows) { dir = -1 }
    if (row == 1) { dir = 1 }
    row = row + dir
  }
  rowStrs.join("")
}
println(zigzagEncode("PAYPALISHIRING", 3))
```

Implement `mostFrequentChar(s)` returning the character that appears most often in `s`. Ignore spaces. If there's a tie, return the one that appears first in the string.
```falcon
func mostFrequentChar(s) = {
 
local freq = {}
 
local order = []
  for (i: 1 .. s.textLen()) {
 
local ch = s.segment(i,1)
    if (ch !== " ") {
      freq.set(ch, freq.get(ch, 0) + 1)
      if (!order.containsItem(ch)) { order.add(ch) }
    }
  }
 
local best = 0
 
local bestCh = ""
  for (ch in order) {
    if (freq.get(ch, 0) > best) {
      best = freq.get(ch, 0)
      bestCh = ch
    }
  }
  bestCh
}
println(mostFrequentChar("aabbccdddee"))
```

```falcon
local alphanums = "abcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz0123456789"
    if (ch === " ") {
      result = result _ "-"
    } else if (alphanums.contains(ch)) {
      result = result _ ch
    }
  }
```

```falcon
func encodeStringURL(s) = {

local result = ""
  for (i: 1 .. s.textLen()) {
```

Write `parseCSVLine(line)` that parses a single CSV line respecting quoted fields. Fields enclosed in `"..."` may contain commas; the function should return a list of field strings with quotes removed.
```falcon
func parseCSVLine(line) = {
 
local fields = []
 
local current = ""
 
local inQuotes = false
  for (i: 1 .. line.textLen()) {
 
local ch = line.segment(i,1)
    if (ch === "\"") {
      inQuotes = !inQuotes
    } else if (ch === "," && !inQuotes) {
      fields.add(current)
      current = ""
    } else {
      current = current _ ch
    }
  }
  fields.add(current)
  fields
}
println(parseCSVLine("name,\"Smith, John\",age,\"30\""))
```

Implement `findRepeatingPattern(s)` that finds the shortest string `p` such that `s` is formed by repeating `p` some number of times. For `"ababab"` return `"ab"`. For `"abcabc"` return `"abc"`. For `"abcd"` return `"abcd"`.
```falcon
func findRepeatingPattern(s) = {
 
local n = s.textLen()
 
local result = s
  for (len: 1 .. n / 2) {
    if (n % len == 0) {
 
local pat = s.segment(1, len)
 
local repeated = ""
      for (k: 1 .. n / len) { repeated = repeated _ pat }
      if (repeated === s) {
        result = pat
        len = n
      }
    }
  }
  result
}
println(findRepeatingPattern("ababab"))
println(findRepeatingPattern("abcabc"))
println(findRepeatingPattern("abcd"))
```

Implement `jaroSimilarity(s1, s2)` computing the Jaro similarity. Find matching characters (within `floor(max(len)/2) - 1` window) and transpositions, then apply the Jaro formula. Print for `"MARTHA"` vs `"MARHTA"`.
```falcon
func jaroSimilarity(s1, s2) = {
 
local l1 = s1.textLen()
 
local l2 = s2.textLen()
 
local matchWindow = floor((if (l1 > l2) l1 else l2) / 2) - 1
 
local s1matched = []
 
local s2matched = []
  for (i: 1 .. l1) { s1matched.add(false) }
  for (i: 1 .. l2) { s2matched.add(false) }
 
local matches = 0
  for (i: 1 .. l1) {
 
local lo = if (i - matchWindow > 1) i - matchWindow else 1
 
local hi = if (i + matchWindow < l2) i + matchWindow else l2
    for (j: lo .. hi) {
      if (!s2matched[j] && !s1matched[i] && s1.segment(i,1) === s2.segment(j,1)) {
        s1matched[i] = true
        s2matched[j] = true
        matches = matches + 1
      }
    }
  }
  if (matches == 0) {
    0
  } else {
 
local trans = 0
 
local k = 1
    for (i: 1 .. l1) {
      if (s1matched[i]) {
        while (k <= l2 && !s2matched[k]) { k = k + 1 }
        if (k <= l2 && s1.segment(i,1) !== s2.segment(k,1)) { trans = trans + 1 }
        k = k + 1
      }
    }
    (matches/l1 + matches/l2 + (matches - trans/2)/matches) / 3
  }
}
println(jaroSimilarity("MARTHA","MARHTA"))
```

Write `longestSubstringKDistinct(s, k)` returning the length of the longest substring containing at most `k` distinct characters. Use a sliding window and a character count dict.
```falcon
func longestSubstringKDistinct(s, k) = {
 
local freq = {}
 
local left = 1
 
local maxLen = 0
  for (right: 1 .. s.textLen()) {
 
local ch = s.segment(right,1)
    freq.set(ch, freq.get(ch, 0) + 1)
    while (freq.dictLen() > k) {
 
local lch = s.segment(left,1)
      freq.set(lch, freq.get(lch, 0) - 1)
      if (freq.get(lch, 0) == 0) { freq.delete(lch) }
      left = left + 1
    }
    if (right - left + 1 > maxLen) { maxLen = right - left + 1 }
  }
  maxLen
}
println(longestSubstringKDistinct("eceba", 2))
```

Implement `compressZlib(s)` using LZ77-style compression: scan left to right, replacing repeated substrings with `(offset, length)` back-references. Return a list alternating between literal characters and reference tuples.
```falcon
func compressZlib(s) = {
 
local result = []
 
local i = 1
  while (i <= s.textLen()) {
 
local bestLen = 0
 
local bestOff = 0
    for (j: 1 .. i - 1) {
 
local len = 0
      while (i + len <= s.textLen() && s.segment(j+len,1) === s.segment(i+len,1) && len < 10) {
        len = len + 1
      }
      if (len > bestLen) {
        bestLen = len
        bestOff = i - j
      }
    }
    if (bestLen >= 3) {
      result.add([bestOff, bestLen])
      i = i + bestLen
    } else {
      result.add(s.segment(i,1))
      i = i + 1
    }
  }
  result
}
println(compressZlib("abcabcabc"))
```

Write `extractMarkdown(text)` that parses simple Markdown: detect `**bold**` (wrap in `[BOLD]`), `_italic_` (wrap in `[ITALIC]`), and `# Heading` (wrap in `[H1]`). Return the converted text.
```falcon
func extractMarkdown(text) = {
 
local result = text
 
local i = 1
 
local out = ""
  while (i <= result.textLen()) {
    if (i <= result.textLen() - 1 && result.segment(i,2) === "**") {
 
local end = i + 2
 
local content = ""
      while (end <= result.textLen() - 1 && result.segment(end,2) !== "**") {
        content = content _ result.segment(end,1)
        end = end + 1
      }
      out = out _ "[BOLD]" _ content _ "[/BOLD]"
      i = end + 2
    } else if (result.segment(i,1) === "_") {
 
local end = i + 1
 
local content = ""
      while (end <= result.textLen() && result.segment(end,1) !== "_") {
        content = content _ result.segment(end,1)
        end = end + 1
      }
      out = out _ "[ITALIC]" _ content _ "[/ITALIC]"
      i = end + 1
    } else if (i == 1 && result.segment(i,2) === "# ") {
      out = "[H1]" _ result.segment(3, result.textLen()-2) _ "[/H1]"
      i = result.textLen() + 1
    } else {
      out = out _ result.segment(i,1)
      i = i + 1
    }
  }
  out
}
println(extractMarkdown("**bold** and _italic_ text"))
println(extractMarkdown("# Heading"))
```

Implement `countSyllables(word)` estimating syllable count: count vowel groups (consecutive vowels count as one), subtract 1 if the word ends in silent `e`, with minimum of 1. Test `"beautiful"` → 3, `"cake"` → 1.
```falcon
func countSyllables(word) = {
 
local vowels = "aeiouAEIOU"
 
local count = 0
 
local prevVowel = false
 
local w = word.lowercase()
  for (i: 1 .. w.textLen()) {
 
local isVowel = vowels.contains(w.segment(i,1))
    if (isVowel && !prevVowel) { count = count + 1 }
    prevVowel = isVowel
  }
  if (w.textLen() > 1 && w.segment(w.textLen(),1) === "e") {
    count = count - 1
  }
  if (count < 1) { 1 } else { count }
}
println(countSyllables("beautiful"))
println(countSyllables("cake"))
```

Implement `stemWord(word)` applying basic Porter stemmer rules: remove trailing `"ing"`, `"ed"`, `"ly"`, `"er"` suffixes if the remaining stem is at least 3 characters. Return the stemmed word.
```falcon
func stemWord(word) = {
 
local suffixes = ["ing","ed","ly","er"]
 
local result = word
  for (suf in suffixes) {
 
local slen = suf.textLen()
 
local wlen = word.textLen()
    if (wlen > slen + 3 && word.segment(wlen - slen + 1, slen) === suf) {
      result = word.segment(1, wlen - slen)
      suf = suffixes[suffixes.listLen()]
    }
  }
  result
}
println(stemWord("running"))
println(stemWord("jumped"))
println(stemWord("quickly"))
```

Write `detectEncoding(s)` that checks if a string appears to be base64 (only contains base64 characters and length divisible by 4), hexadecimal (only 0-9a-f), or plain ASCII. Return `"base64"`, `"hex"`, or `"ascii"`.
```falcon
func detectEncoding(s) = {
 
local b64chars = "ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZabcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz0123456789+/="
 
local hexchars = "0123456789abcdefABCDEF"
 
local isB64 = s.textLen() % 4 == 0
 
local isHex = true
 
local isB64Valid = true
  for (i: 1 .. s.textLen()) {
 
local ch = s.segment(i,1)
    if (!b64chars.contains(ch)) { isB64Valid = false }
    if (!hexchars.contains(ch)) { isHex = false }
  }
  if (isB64 && isB64Valid) {
    "base64"
  } else if (isHex) {
    "hex"
  } else {
    "ascii"
  }
}
println(detectEncoding("SGVsbG8="))
println(detectEncoding("1a2b3c"))
println(detectEncoding("Hello World"))
```

Implement `intToRoman(n)` converting a positive integer to a Roman numeral. Use a list of `(value, symbol)` pairs including all subtraction pairs. Test `n = 3749` → `"MMMDCCXLIX"`.
```falcon
func intToRoman(n) = {
 
local vals = [1000,900,500,400,100,90,50,40,10,9,5,4,1]
 
local syms = ["M","CM","D","CD","C","XC","L","XL","X","IX","V","IV","I"]
 
local result = ""
 
local num = n
  for (i: 1 .. vals.listLen()) {
    while (num >= vals[i]) {
      result = result _ syms[i]
      num = num - vals[i]
    }
  }
  result
}
println(intToRoman(3749))
```

Implement `expandCamelCase(s)` that converts a camelCase string to space-separated words. For `"camelCaseString"` return `"camel Case String"` and for `"XMLParser"` return `"X M L Parser"`.
```falcon
func expandCamelCase(s) = {
 
local result = s.segment(1,1)
 
local uppers = "ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZ"
  for (i: 2 .. s.textLen()) {
 
local ch = s.segment(i,1)
    if (uppers.contains(ch)) {
      result = result _ " " _ ch
    } else {
      result = result _ ch
    }
  }
  result
}
println(expandCamelCase("camelCaseString"))
println(expandCamelCase("XMLParser"))
```

Write `topKFrequent(words, k)` taking a list of words and returning the `k` most frequent ones. Use a frequency dict, then select top `k` by count. Return only the words (not counts).
```falcon
func topKFrequent(words, k) = {
 
local freq = {}
  for (w in words) {
    freq.set(w, freq.get(w, 0) + 1)
  }
 
local pairs = []
  for (w, c in freq) { pairs.add([c, w]) }
  pairs.sort()
  pairs.reverseList()
 
local result = []
  for (i: 1 .. k) {
    if (i <= pairs.listLen()) { result.add(pairs[i][2]) }
  }
  result
}
println(topKFrequent(["apple","banana","apple","cherry","banana","apple"], 2))
```

```falcon
    result.get(fc, []).add(word)
```

Write `buildWordIndex(sentences)` where `sentences` is a list of strings. Return a dict mapping each word to a list of `[sentenceIndex, wordPosition]` pairs (both 1-based).

```falcon
func buildWordIndex(sentences) = {
 
local index = {}
  for (si: 1 .. sentences.listLen()) {
 
local words = sentences[si].splitAtSpaces()
    for (wi: 1 .. words.listLen()) {
 
local w = words[wi]
      if (!index.containsKey(w)) { index.set(w, []) }
      index.get(w, []).add([si, wi])
    }
  }

}
```

Write `longestNonRepeatingList(words)` finding the longest sublist of `words` containing all distinct strings. Use a sliding window with a dict tracking current window contents.
```falcon
func longestNonRepeatingList(words) = {
 
local freq = {}
 
local left = 1
 
local maxLen = 0
  for (right: 1 .. words.listLen()) {
 
local w = words[right]
    freq.set(w, freq.get(w, 0) + 1)
    while (freq.get(w, 0) > 1) {
 
local lw = words[left]
      freq.set(lw, freq.get(lw, 0) - 1)
      if (freq.get(lw, 0) == 0) { freq.delete(lw) }
      left = left + 1
    }
    if (right - left + 1 > maxLen) { maxLen = right - left + 1 }
  }
  maxLen
}
println(longestNonRepeatingList(["a","b","c","a","b","d","e"]))
```

Write `countUniqueSubstrings(chars, suffixArr, lcpArr)` using the formula `n*(n+1)/2 - sum(lcpArr)` to count distinct non-empty substrings of the character list.
```falcon
func countUniqueSubstrings(chars, suffixArr, lcpArr) = {
 
local n = chars.listLen()
 
local total = n * (n + 1) / 2
 
local lcpSum = lcpArr.reduce(0) { x, acc -> x + acc }
  total - lcpSum
}
println(countUniqueSubstrings("abc".split(""), [1,2,3], [0,0,1]))
```

Write `buildBagOfWords(sentences)` where `sentences` is a list of strings. Return a dict mapping each unique word (across all sentences) to a list of per-sentence counts.
```falcon
func buildBagOfWords(sentences) = {
 
local allWords = []
 
local tokenized = []
  for (s in sentences) {
 
local words = s.splitAtSpaces()
    tokenized.add(words)
    for (w in words) {
      if (!allWords.containsItem(w)) { allWords.add(w) }
    }
  }
 
local result = {}
  for (w in allWords) {
 
local counts = []
    for (ws in tokenized) {
 
local cnt = 0
      for (tw in ws) { if (tw === w) { cnt = cnt + 1 } }
      counts.add(cnt)
    }
    result.set(w, counts)
  }
  result
}
println(buildBagOfWords(["the cat sat","the dog ran","cat and dog"]))
```

Write `buildAnagramBuckets(words)` returning a list of lists where each inner list contains all anagrams of each other from `words`. Words not sharing anagrams with anyone else are in singleton lists.
```falcon
func buildAnagramBuckets(words) = {
 
local groups = {}
  for (word in words) {
 
local chars = word.split("")
    chars.sort()
 
local key = chars.join("")
    if (!groups.containsKey(key)) { groups.set(key, []) }
    groups.get(key, []).add(word)
  }
  groups.values()
}
println(buildAnagramBuckets(["eat","tea","tan","ate","nat","bat","cat"]))
```

Write `detectPatternList(chars, pattern)` where both `chars` and `pattern` are lists of characters. Return all 1-based starting positions of `pattern` in `chars` using naive matching.
```falcon
func detectPatternList(chars, pattern) = {
 
local plen = pattern.listLen()
 
local clen = chars.listLen()
 
local matches = []
  for (i: 1 .. clen - plen + 1) {
 
local ok = true
    for (j: 1 .. plen) {
      if (chars[i+j-1] !== pattern[j]) { ok = false }
    }
    if (ok) { matches.add(i) }
  }
  matches
}
println(detectPatternList("abcabcabc".split(""), "abc".split("")))
```

Write `getAllMatchesMultiPattern(text, patternList)` returning a dict mapping each pattern to its list of match positions in `text`. Process each pattern independently.
```falcon
func getAllMatchesMultiPattern(text, patternList) = {
 
local result = {}
  for (pattern in patternList) {
 
local positions = []
 
local plen = pattern.textLen()
    for (i: 1 .. text.textLen() - plen + 1) {
      if (text.segment(i, plen) === pattern) { positions.add(i) }
    }
    result.set(pattern, positions)
  }
  result
}
```

Implement `buildStringStats(words)` returning a dict with keys `"minLen"`, `"maxLen"`, `"avgLen"`, `"totalChars"`, `"uniqueChars"` (count of distinct characters across all words), and `"mostCommonWord"`.
```falcon
func buildStringStats(words) = {
 
local minLen = 999999
 
local maxLen = 0
 
local totalChars = 0
 
local allChars = {}
 
local freqW = {}
  for (w in words) {
 
local l = w.textLen()
    if (l < minLen) { minLen = l }
    if (l > maxLen) { maxLen = l }
    totalChars = totalChars + l
    freqW.set(w, freqW.get(w, 0) + 1)
    for (i: 1 .. l) { allChars.set(w.segment(i,1), true) }
  }
 
local best = 0
 
local bestWord = ""
  for (w, c in freqW) {
    if (c > best) { best = c bestWord = w }
  }
  {"minLen": minLen, "maxLen": maxLen, "avgLen": totalChars / words.listLen(), "totalChars": totalChars, "uniqueChars": allChars.dictLen(), "mostCommonWord": bestWord}
}
println(buildStringStats(["apple","banana","apple","cherry","banana","apple"]))
```

Write `buildDictionaryIndex(entries)` where `entries` is a list of `{"word":w,"definition":d}` dicts. Build and return an inverted index mapping each word in definitions to the list of dictionary words whose definitions contain it.
```falcon
func buildDictionaryIndex(entries) = {
 
local index = {}
  for (entry in entries) {
 
local word = entry.get("word","")
 
local def = entry.get("definition","")
 
local defWords = def.splitAtSpaces()
    for (dw in defWords) {
 
local dw2 = dw.lowercase()
      if (!index.containsKey(dw2)) { index.set(dw2, []) }
      if (!index.get(dw2, []).containsItem(word)) {
        index.get(dw2, []).add(word)
      }
    }
  }
  index
}
```

Write `buildRegexDFA(pattern)` for patterns with only `a-z` literals and `?` wildcard. Return a dict-based DFA (states and transitions) for the pattern `"a?c"`. Represent states as integers with dict of char→next_state transitions.
```falcon
func buildRegexDFA(pattern) = {
 
local states = {}
 
local n = pattern.textLen()
  for (i: 0 .. n) { states.set(i, {}) }
  for (i: 1 .. n) {
 
local ch = pattern.segment(i,1)
    if (ch === "?") {
 
local alpha = "abcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz"
      for (j: 1 .. 26) {
        states.get(i-1, {}).set(alpha.segment(j,1), i)
      }
    } else {
      states.get(i-1, {}).set(ch, i)
    }
  }
  {"start": 0, "accept": [n], "transitions": states}
}
println(buildRegexDFA("a?c"))
```

Implement `buildDependencyDict(text)` for a simple rule-based parser: map each noun to the verb it's closest to in the sentence, creating a dict of noun→verb dependency pairs.
```falcon
func buildDependencyDict(text) = {
 
local words = text.splitAtSpaces()
 
local verbs = ["is","are","was","were","run","runs","eat","eats","sits","sat","jumped","sees"]
 
local result = {}
  for (i: 1 .. words.listLen()) {
 
local w = words[i].lowercase()
    if (!verbs.containsItem(w)) {
 
local closestVerb = ""
 
local closestDist = 999999
      for (j: 1 .. words.listLen()) {
 
local v = words[j].lowercase()
        if (verbs.containsItem(v)) {
 
local dist = abs(i - j)
          if (dist < closestDist) {
            closestDist = dist
            closestVerb = v
          }
        }
      }
      if (closestVerb !== "") { result.set(w, closestVerb) }
    }
  }
  result
}
println(buildDependencyDict("The cat sits on the mat"))
```

Implement `buildStopWordFilter(texts, threshold)` automatically identifying stop words: words appearing in more than `threshold` fraction of texts. Return a dict of stop_word→fraction pairs.
```falcon
func buildStopWordFilter(texts, threshold) = {
 
local wordInText = {}
 
local n = texts.listLen()
  for (text in texts) {
 
local words = text.splitAtSpaces()
 
local seen = []
    for (w in words) {
 
local wl = w.lowercase()
      if (!seen.containsItem(wl)) {
        seen.add(wl)
        wordInText.set(wl, wordInText.get(wl, 0) + 1)
      }
    }
  }
 
local result = {}
  for (w, cnt in wordInText) {
 
local frac = cnt / n
    if (frac > threshold) { result.set(w, frac) }
  }
  result
}
println(buildStopWordFilter(["the cat sat","the dog ran","the bird flew","a cat flew"], 0.5))
```

Implement `buildChunkDict(text, delimiters)` splitting text on multiple delimiters (provided as a list of strings) and counting the frequency of each segment. Return a dict of segment→count.
```falcon
func buildChunkDict(text, delimiters) = {
 
local segments = [text]
  for (delim in delimiters) {
 
local newSegs = []
    for (seg in segments) {
 
local parts = seg.split(delim)
      for (p in parts) {
 
local pt = p.trim()
        if (pt.textLen() > 0) { newSegs.add(pt) }
      }
    }
    segments = newSegs
  }
 
local result = {}
  for (seg in segments) {
    result.set(seg, result.get(seg, 0) + 1)
  }
  result
}
println(buildChunkDict("hello world, foo bar hello foo", [",",""," "]))
```

Implement a program using `for` loops to build a suffix automaton for `"abbc"`: create states and transitions iteratively. Print the number of states (should be at most `2n-1`).
```falcon
local saWord = "abbc"
local saLen = saWord.textLen()
local saStates = [{}]
local saLast = 1
local saSize = 1
for (ci: 1 .. saLen) {
 
local ch = saWord.segment(ci, 1)
 
local cur = saSize + 1
  saStates.add({})
  saSize = saSize + 1
 
local p = saLast
  while (p != 0 && !saStates[p].containsKey(ch)) {
    saStates[p].set(ch, cur)
    p = saStates[p].get("link", 0)
  }
  saLast = cur
}
println("States: " _ saStates.listLen())
```

Implement a `while` loop for base-64 encoding of `"Man"`: convert each character to its ASCII value, combine 3 bytes into 24 bits, split into four 6-bit values, map to base64 alphabet characters.
```falcon
local b64input = "Man"
local b64alpha = "ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZabcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz0123456789+/"
local asciiMap = {}
local allChars = " !\"#$%&'()*+,-./0123456789:<=>?@ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZ[\\]^_`abcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz{|}~"
for (i: 1 .. allChars.textLen()) {
  asciiMap.set(allChars.segment(i,1), i + 31)
}
local bytes = []
for (i: 1 .. b64input.textLen()) {
  bytes.add(asciiMap.get(b64input.segment(i,1, []), 0))
}
local bits = 0
for (b in bytes) { bits = bits * 256 + b }
local result3645 = ""
local indices = []
for (i: 1 .. 4) {
  indices.add(bits % 64)
  bits = floor(bits / 64)
}
indices.reverseList()
for (idx in indices) {
```

Implement `func sentenceSegment(text) = { ... }` splitting text into sentences using heuristic rules: split on `.`, `!`, `?` but not on abbreviations (detected by single uppercase letter before `.`). Return a list of sentences.
```falcon
func sentenceSegment(text) = {
 
local sentences = []
 
local current = ""
 
local i = 1
  while (i <= text.textLen()) {
 
local ch = text.segment(i,1)
    current = current _ ch
    if (ch === "." || ch === "!" || ch === "?") {
 
local isAbbrev = false
      if (ch === "." && i >= 2) {
 
local prev = text.segment(i-1,1)
 
local uppers = "ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZ"
        if (uppers.contains(prev) && (i < 3 || text.segment(i-2,1) === " ")) {
          isAbbrev = true
        }
      }
      if (!isAbbrev) {
        sentences.add(current.trim())
        current = ""
      }
    }
    i = i + 1
  }
  if (current.trim().textLen() > 0) { sentences.add(current.trim()) }
  sentences
}
println(sentenceSegment("Hello world. How are you? I am fine! Dr. Smith said hello."))
```

Find the longest increasing subsequence length in `[3,1,4,1,5,9,2,6,5,3,5]` using a dynamic programming approach. Print the length (expected: `4`, e.g., 1,4,5,6 or 1,2,5,5... actually 1,4,5,9 has length 4).
```falcon
local seq3981 = [3,1,4,1,5,9,2,6,5,3,5]
local dp3981 = []
for (i: 1 .. seq3981.listLen()) { dp3981.add(1) }
for (i: 2 .. seq3981.listLen()) {
  for (j: 1 .. i-1) {
    if (seq3981[j] < seq3981[i] && dp3981[j] + 1 > dp3981[i]) {
      dp3981[i] = dp3981[j] + 1
    }
  }
}
local maxLIS = dp3981.reduce(0) { x, acc -> if (x > acc) x else acc }
println(maxLIS)
```

Implement insertion sort on `[5,2,4,6,1,3]` and track each intermediate state as a list of lists. Print the final sorted list `[1,2,3,4,5,6]`.
```falcon
local arr4009 = [5,2,4,6,1,3]
local states = [copyList(arr4009)]
for (i: 2 .. arr4009.listLen()) {
 
local key = arr4009[i]
 
local j = i - 1
  while (j >= 1 && arr4009[j] > key) {
    arr4009[j+1] = arr4009[j]
    j = j - 1
  }
  arr4009[j+1] = key
  states.add(copyList(arr4009))
}
println(arr4009)
```

Write a function `func isPrime(n) = { ... }` that returns `1` if `n` is prime and `0` otherwise using trial division. Test with `isPrime(97)` and `isPrime(100)`. Print `"97: prime, 100: composite"`.

```falcon
func isPrime(n) = {
```

Write a function `func collatzLen(n) = { ... }` returning the length of the Collatz sequence starting at `n`. Use a loop counting steps until reaching 1. Compute `collatzLen(27)`. Print `112` (including start value 27 to end value 1).
```falcon
func collatzLen(n) = {
 
local count = 1
 
local x = n
  while (x != 1) {
    if (x % 2 == 0) { x = x / 2 } else { x = 3 * x + 1 }
    count = count + 1
  }
  count
}
println(collatzLen(27))
```

Write a function `func primeFactors(n) = { ... }` returning a list of prime factors (with multiplicity). Compute `primeFactors(360)`. Print `[2,2,2,3,3,5]`.
```falcon
func primeFactors(n) = {
 
local factors = []
 
local x = n
 
local d = 2
  while (d * d <= x) {
    while (x % d == 0) {
      factors.add(d)
      x = floor(x / d)
    }
    d = d + 1
  }
  if (x > 1) { factors.add(x) }
  factors
}
println(primeFactors(360))
```

Write a function `func isHappy(n) = { ... }` using a `while` loop with a seen-dict to detect cycles. If the sequence reaches 1, return `1`; if it enters the unhappy cycle (containing 4), return `0`. Test `isHappy(7)`. Print `"7 is happy"`.
```falcon
func isHappy(n) = {
 
local seen = {}
 
local x = n
  while (x != 1 && !seen.containsKey(x)) {
    seen.set(x, true)
 
local digits = ("" _ x).split("")
 
local sum = 0
    for (d in digits) { sum = sum + d * d }
    x = sum
  }
  if (x == 1) { 1 } else { 0 }
}
println("7 is " _ if (isHappy(7) == 1) "happy" else "unhappy")
```

Write a function `func numberToRoman(n) = { ... }` converting a number to Roman numerals using a loop over value-symbol pairs. Compute `numberToRoman(2024)`. Print `"MMXXIV"`.
```falcon
func numberToRoman(n) = {
 
local vals = [1000,900,500,400,100,90,50,40,10,9,5,4,1]
 
local syms = ["M","CM","D","CD","C","XC","L","XL","X","IX","V","IV","I"]
 
local result = ""
 
local num = n
  for (i: 1 .. vals.listLen()) {
    while (num >= vals[i]) {
      result = result _ syms[i]
      num = num - vals[i]
    }
  }
  result
}
println(numberToRoman(2024))
```

Compute the product of all prime factors of `2310` without repetition (the radical). Print the result (expected: `2310`, since 2310=2*3*5*7*11 and all factors appear once).
```falcon
local n3759 = 2310
local x3759 = n3759
local radical = 1
local d3759 = 2
local seenFactors = []
while (d3759 * d3759 <= x3759) {
  if (x3759 % d3759 == 0) {
    if (!seenFactors.containsItem(d3759)) {
      radical = radical * d3759
      seenFactors.add(d3759)
    }
    while (x3759 % d3759 == 0) { x3759 = floor(x3759 / d3759) }
  }
  d3759 = d3759 + 1
}
if (x3759 > 1 && !seenFactors.containsItem(x3759)) { radical = radical * x3759 }
println(radical)
```

Determine if `6601` is a strong pseudoprime to base 2 (passes Miller-Rabin with base 2 but is composite). Write the Miller-Rabin test for `n=6601, a=2`. Print `"Pseudoprime"` if it passes the test.
```falcon
local n3773 = 6601
local a3773 = 2
local nm1 = n3773 - 1
local r3773 = 0
local d3773m = nm1
while (d3773m % 2 == 0) {
  d3773m = floor(d3773m / 2)
  r3773 = r3773 + 1
}
local x3773 = 1
local base3773 = a3773
local exp3773 = d3773m
while (exp3773 > 0) {
  if (exp3773 % 2 == 1) { x3773 = mod(x3773 * base3773, n3773) }
  base3773 = mod(base3773 * base3773, n3773)
  exp3773 = floor(exp3773 / 2)
}
local isPseudo = x3773 == 1 || x3773 == nm1
if (!isPseudo) {
  for (s: 1 .. r3773 - 1) {
    x3773 = mod(x3773 * x3773, n3773)
    if (x3773 == nm1) { isPseudo = true }
  }
}
println(if (isPseudo) "Pseudoprime" else "Composite")
```

Implement the algorithm to find primitive roots modulo a prime `p = 7`. A primitive root `g` satisfies `g^k mod p` cycles through all values 1..p-1. Test `g=3`: 3,2,6,4,5,1 — all distinct. Print `"3 is a primitive root mod 7"`.
```falcon
local p4163 = 7
local g4163 = 3
local powers = []
local cur4163 = 1
for (k: 1 .. p4163 - 1) {
  cur4163 = mod(cur4163 * g4163, p4163)
  powers.add(cur4163)
}
local allDistinct = true
for (i: 1 .. powers.listLen()) {
  for (j: i+1 .. powers.listLen()) {
    if (powers[i] == powers[j]) { allDistinct = false }
  }
}
println(if (allDistinct) "3 is a primitive root mod 7" else "Not a primitive root")
```

Declare `global odious = 0`. An odious number has an odd number of 1-bits in its binary representation. Find the first odious number greater than 10 and store in `odious`. Print `this.odious` (expected: `11`, since 1011 has three 1-bits).
```falcon
global odious = 0
local n3737 = 11
while (this.odious == 0) {
  if (n3737 > 10) {
 
local bits = 0
 
local tmp = n3737
    while (tmp > 0) {
      bits = bits + mod(tmp, 2)
      tmp = floor(tmp / 2)
    }
    if (mod(bits, 2) == 1) { this.odious = n3737 }
  }
  if (this.odious == 0) { n3737 = n3737 + 1 }
}
println(this.odious)
```

Compute `phi(phi(100))` where `phi` is Euler's totient function. First compute `phi(100) = 40`, then `phi(40) = 16`. Print `16`.
```falcon
func eulerPhi(n) = {
 
local result = n
 
local x = n
 
local d = 2
  while (d * d <= x) {
    if (x % d == 0) {
      while (x % d == 0) { x = floor(x / d) }
      result = result - floor(result / d)
    }
    d = d + 1
  }
  if (x > 1) { result = result - floor(result / x) }
  result
}
println(eulerPhi(eulerPhi(100)))
```

Find the smallest positive integer that is divisible by all integers from 1 to 20 (lcm(1..20)). Print the result (expected: `232792560`).
```falcon
local lcm = 1

local a = lcm
 
local b = i
 
local g = a
 
local h = b
  while (h != 0) {
 
local tmp = h
    h = mod(g, h)
    g = tmp
  }

}
```

Compute the multiplicative digital root of `9875`: 9*8*7*5=2520, 2*5*2*0=0. Print `0`.
```falcon
local n3864 = 9875
while (n3864 >= 10) {
 
local s = "" _ n3864
 
local p = 1
  for (i: 1 .. s.textLen()) { p = p * s.segment(i,1) }
  n3864 = p
}
```

Implement run-length encoding: compress `"aaabbbccddddee"` → `"3a3b2c4d2e"`. Print the encoded string.
```falcon
local rle = "aaabbbccddddee"
local encoded = ""
local i3886 = 1
while (i3886 <= rle.textLen()) {
 
local ch = rle.segment(i3886, 1)
 
local count = 1
  while (i3886 + count <= rle.textLen() && rle.segment(i3886+count, 1) === ch) {
    count = count + 1
  }
  encoded = encoded _ count _ ch
  i3886 = i3886 + count
}
```

Write a `while` loop that implements the binary GCD algorithm: for `a=48, b=36`, repeatedly use the relation `gcd(a,b) = gcd(b, mod(a,b))` until `b=0`. Print `gcd(48,36)=12`.

Use a `for` loop to find all `n` from 1 to 1000 where `mod(2^n, n) = 1` (Fermat-like condition). Count and print the count.
```falcon
local count4131 = 0
for (n: 1 .. 1000) {
 
local pow2 = 1
 
local base = 2
 
local exp = n
  while (exp > 0) {
    if (mod(exp, 2) == 1) { pow2 = mod(pow2 * base, n + 1) }
    base = mod(base * base, n + 1)
    exp = floor(exp / 2)
  }
  if (mod(pow2, n) == 1) { count4131 = count4131 + 1 }
}
println(count4131)
```

Use a `for` loop to find all numbers from 1 to 1000 that are simultaneously triangular and pentagonal (pentagonal numbers: `n*(3n-1)/2`). Print the count and the values found.
```falcon
local triPentList = []
local triPentCount = 0
for (n: 1 .. 1000) {
 
local tri = n * (n+1) / 2
 
local sqrtTerm = sqrt(1 + 24*tri)
  if (mod(round(sqrtTerm), 1) == 0) {
 
local m = round(sqrtTerm)
    if (m * m == 1 + 24*tri && mod(m + 1, 6) == 0) {
 
local k = (m + 1) / 6
 
local pent = k * (3*k - 1) / 2
      if (pent == tri) {
        triPentList.add(tri)
        triPentCount = triPentCount + 1
      }
    }
  }
}
```

Compute the Jacobi symbol `(a/n)` for `a=286, n=221` using the recursive definition. Print the result (expected: `-1`).
```falcon
func jacobiSymbol(a, n) = {
  if (n == 1) { 1 }
  else {
 
local result = 1
 
local x = mod(a, n)
 
local m = n
    while (x != 0) {
      while (mod(x, 2) == 0) {
        x = floor(x / 2)
 
local r = mod(m, 8)
        if (r == 3 || r == 5) { result = -result }
      }
 
local tmp = m
      m = x
      x = tmp
      if (mod(x, 4) == 3 && mod(m, 4) == 3) { result = -result }
      x = mod(x, m)
    }
    if (m == 1) { result } else { 0 }
  }
}
println(jacobiSymbol(286, 221))
```

```falcon
local hashStr = "hello"

for (i: 1 .. hashStr.textLen()) {

local code = 0
  for (j: 1 .. hashAlpha.textLen()) {
    if (hashAlpha.segment(j,1) === ch) { code = j + 31 }
```

Given a list of 3D triangles, sort them by their centroid's z-coordinate for painter's algorithm (back-to-front rendering order).

```falcon
local triangles = [{"verts":[[0,0,5],[1,0,5],[0,1,5]]},{"verts":[[0,0,1],[1,0,1],[0,1,1]]},{"verts":[[0,0,3],[1,0,3],[0,1,3]]}]
```

Write a `while` loop for the A* path search: continue while the open set is non-empty, each iteration extracting the minimum-f-cost node and expanding its neighbors.
```falcon
func astarSearch(graph, start, goal, heuristic) = {
 
local openSet = [[heuristic.get(start,0), 0, start, [start]]]
 
local visited = []
 
local result = []
  while (openSet.listLen() > 0) {
    openSet.sort()
 
local cur = openSet[1]
    openSet.remove(1)
 
local f = cur[1]
 
local g = cur[2]
 
local node = cur[3]
 
local path = cur[4]
    if (node === goal) {
      result = path
      openSet = []
    } else if (!visited.containsItem(node)) {
      visited.add(node)
      for (nb, w in graph.get(node, {})) {
        if (!visited.containsItem(nb)) {
 
local ng = g + w
 
local nf = ng + heuristic.get(nb, 0)
 
local np = copyList(path)
          np.add(nb)
          openSet.add([nf, ng, nb, np])
        }
      }
    }
  }
  result
}
local g = {"A":{"B":1,"C":4},"B":{"C":2,"D":5},"C":{"D":1},"D":{}}
local h = {"A":4,"B":2,"C":1,"D":0}
println(astarSearch(g, "A", "D", h))
```

Normalize all points in a list to fit within `[0,1] x [0,1]` by computing the bounding box and remapping each coordinate linearly.
```falcon
func normalizePoints(points) = {
 
local minX = points[1][1]
 
local minY = points[1][2]
 
local maxX = points[1][1]
 
local maxY = points[1][2]
  for (p in points) {
    if (p[1] < minX) { minX = p[1] }
    if (p[2] < minY) { minY = p[2] }
    if (p[1] > maxX) { maxX = p[1] }
    if (p[2] > maxY) { maxY = p[2] }
  }
 
local rx = if (maxX == minX) 1 else maxX - minX
 
local ry = if (maxY == minY) 1 else maxY - minY
  points.map { p -> [(p[1]-minX)/rx, (p[2]-minY)/ry] }
}
println(normalizePoints([[0,0],[10,5],[5,10],[10,10]]))
```

Write a returning function `func rotate2D(pt, theta) = { ... }` that rotates a 2D point `[x,y]` around the origin by `theta` radians and returns the new point.
```falcon
func rotate2D(pt, theta) = {
 
local x = pt[1]
 
local y = pt[2]
 
local listResult = [x*cos(theta) - y*sin(theta), x*sin(theta) + y*cos(theta)]
  listResult
}
println(rotate2D([1,0], radians(90)))
```

Write a returning function `func bezierPoint(controls, t) = { ... }` that evaluates a cubic Bezier curve at parameter `t` given four control points, returning the `[x,y]` position.
```falcon
func bezierPoint(controls, t) = {
 
local p0 = controls[1]
 
local p1 = controls[2]
 
local p2 = controls[3]
 
local p3 = controls[4]
 
local mt = 1 - t
 
local x = mt^3*p0[1] + 3*mt^2*t*p1[1] + 3*mt*t^2*p2[1] + t^3*p3[1]
 
local y = mt^3*p0[2] + 3*mt^2*t*p1[2] + 3*mt*t^2*p2[2] + t^3*p3[2]
 
local listResult = [x, y]
  listResult
}
println(bezierPoint([[0,0],[1,2],[2,2],[3,0]], 0.5))
```

Write a returning function `func computeSDF(polygons, queryPt) = { ... }` that computes the signed distance from `queryPt` to the nearest polygon boundary in a list of polygons: positive if outside all polygons, negative if inside any polygon.
```falcon
func computeSDF(polygons, queryPt) = {
 
local minDist = 999999
 
local inside = false
  for (poly in polygons) {
 
local n = poly.listLen()
 
local isInside = false
 
local px = queryPt[1]
 
local py = queryPt[2]
 
local j = n
    for (i: 1 .. n) {
 
local xi = poly[i][1]
 
local yi = poly[i][2]
 
local xj = poly[j][1]
 
local yj = poly[j][2]
      if (((yi > py) != (yj > py)) && px < (xj-xi)*(py-yi)/(yj-yi)+xi) {
        isInside = !isInside
      }
 
local dx = poly[i][1] - px
 
local dy = poly[i][2] - py
 
local d = sqrt(dx*dx+dy*dy)
      if (d < minDist) { minDist = d }
      j = i
    }
    if (isInside) { inside = true }
  }
  if (inside) { -minDist } else { minDist }
}
println(computeSDF([[[0,0],[4,0],[4,4],[0,4]]], [2,2]))
println(computeSDF([[[0,0],[4,0],[4,4],[0,4]]], [6,6]))
```
